Theorem 219.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The semiconductors variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 889 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 476 and intersection form of rank 4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to semiconductors theory, we decompose the problem into 889 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 476. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 219.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on semiconductors structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 493. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 410 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the semiconductors decomposition. By minimality, the element a^493 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 410-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
